# Awesome Jev

[![Awesome](https://awesome.re/badge.svg)](https://awesome.re)
[![PRs Welcome](https://img.shields.io/badge/PRs-welcome-brightgreen.svg)](https://github.com/robokrunch/awesome-jev/pulls)

A curated list of resources for **Jev** — TypeSafe AI's "System One" model that returns typed, calibrated decisions instead of text.

Jev doesn't chat. You send it structured state; it returns a probability distribution over your predefined options in 70–500ms, at **$0.042 per million input tokens with output free**. It was built by Diogo Almeida (ex-OpenAI, co-inventor of RLHF/ChatGPT), emerged from stealth in September 2026 with $40M in funding, and **went generally available on 2026-09-21**: no waitlist, $5 starter credits (~120M tokens), with Vercel, Cloudflare, LangChain and Langfuse already adding it to their stacks (per Indian Express).

This list is maintained by [RoboKrunch](https://robokrunch.com) — we benchmark Chinese edge-AI hardware and run our own real-measurement Jev experiments (300 real API calls, real latency, real bills). Entries with our own measured numbers are marked 📊.

> **No official paper.** TypeSafe has not published a formal paper on Jev as of 2026-09-23. If one appears, it goes here first.

## Official

- [typesafe.ai](https://typesafe.ai/) — Official site (generally available since 2026-09-21; $5 starter credits).
- [Quickstart](https://docs.typesafe.ai/introduction/quickstart) — Official docs: `POST /v1/systemone`, question types (`choice` up to 255 options, `noul` yes/no, `score`).
- [@typesafeai on X](https://x.com/typesafeai) — Official announcements.
- [Founder launch thread](https://x.com/CompleteSkeptic/status/2099925682726002904) — Diogo Almeida introduces Jev and RLCD.
- [Jev on OpenRouter](https://openrouter.ai/typesafe/jev-1.13) — `typesafe/jev-1.13`, $0.042/M input, $0/M output, 32K context. Note: OpenRouter exposes Jev via its decisions endpoint, not `/chat/completions`.
- [Jev on Cloudflare](https://developers.cloudflare.com/ai/models/typesafe/jev/) — Workers AI model page.
- [Jev 1.13 on LLM Gateway](https://llmgateway.io/models/jev-1.13.0) — Third-party provider listing: $0.042/M input, $0/M output, 64k context, OpenAI-compatible API with automatic provider routing and fallback.
- [$40M stealth emergence (Business Wire)](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260915525333/en/TypeSafe-AI-Emerges-From-Stealth-With-%2440M-in-Funding-With-New-Model-for-Composable-AI) — Funding announcement, September 2026.

## Models & API

- **Native API**: `POST https://api.typesafe.ai/v1/systemone` — structured DOM/state in, `{operation, target}` style typed decisions out. One call can carry many questions in parallel.
- **Question primitives**: `choice` (pick from up to 255 options, per-option probabilities + `confidence`), `noul` (yes/no as a single probability), `score` (numerical).
- **Pricing**: $0.042 / 1M input tokens, $0 output. No tokens wasted on explanations.
- **Latency envelope**: TypeSafe quotes 70–500ms; independent testers measure p50 ~0.2–0.8s depending on task and route (see [Benchmarks](#benchmarks--evaluations)).

- [mohdyaser/Jev-multimodal](https://github.com/mohdyaser/Jev-multimodal) — Jev-inspired multimodal decision prototype on Gemma 4 E4B: typed decisions from a question plus zero to four ordered images, scoring one-token option markers over the supplied options. Apple Silicon MLX stack with a pinned model manifest, synthetic-only data, and a RESULTS.md local run dated 2026-09-29. Author's own framing: interaction-pattern inspiration only — not Jev's private architecture, no Jev-equivalence claim. (0★, Python, no license declared, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified, RESULTS.md verified)
- [dtheofr/typesafe-jev-ruby](https://github.com/dtheofr/typesafe-jev-ruby) — Ruby client gem for the Jev System One API (0.7.0): `Typesafe::Client#evaluate` posts state + typed questions; `Typesafe::Jev` pins the model to `jev-latest`; typed error subclasses for every non-2xx status; immutable `NoulAnswer` / `ChoiceAnswer` / `ScoreAnswer` objects with probabilities and confidence. Published on RubyGems, CI green. (1★, Ruby, MIT, created 2026-09-20; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, CHANGELOG verified)
- [thehumanworks/jevgrep](https://github.com/thehumanworks/jevgrep) — Rust crate `typesafe-jev` (0.2.0) with the three question types as real Rust types: `Noul`, `Choice` and `Score` question types follow TypeSafe's API reference field-for-field, with matching `NoulAnswer` / `ChoiceAnswer` / `ScoreAnswer` response types, `Question` enum and ordered `Questions` set. Independent of the two `jevgrep` code-search tools listed in Projects & Code (same name, different projects). (1★, Rust, no license declared, created 2026-09-17; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, crate CHANGELOG verified)
- [CMaintz/jev-dotnet](https://github.com/CMaintz/jev-dotnet) — Unofficial dependency-free .NET client for TypeSafe's Jev: send a state + typed questions, get typed answers with calibrated confidence that code can act on (README notes it is not published or endorsed by TypeSafe). (0★, MIT, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [CMaintz/jev-java](https://github.com/CMaintz/jev-java) — Unofficial dependency-free Java client for TypeSafe's Jev (Java 21+): send a state + typed questions, get typed answers with calibrated confidence; README states it is community-built, not published or endorsed by TypeSafe. (0★, Java, MIT, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [ivanblagdan/jev-cli](https://github.com/ivanblagdan/jev-cli) — Composable Rust CLI for TypeSafe's Jev: `choice`, `score`, `noul`; read state from stdin, ask one typed question per state, streaming JSON to stdout. `jev auth set-key` stores the key in the OS credential store (macOS Keychain / Windows Credential Manager / Linux Secret Service), no plaintext fallback. (0★, Rust, no license declared, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [PostHog/jeeves](https://github.com/PostHog/jeeves) — Reasoning Jev-style classifier from PostHog: 9B Jev-like model (Qwen3.5-9B, LoRA, pointer head) with a block-4 diffusion drafter, trained with SFT and CISPO to "think before it decides". Author's numbers: beats Kev-9B and Jev on held-out test data (0.889 vs 0.822 / 0.857) and on JevBench public tiers (0.935 vs 0.866 for Jev; hard tier 0.865 vs 0.730). Jev-compatible API (noul/choice/score in one request), full training code + train/dev/test data in-repo, weights on HuggingFace. ~0.3s/request without thinking, 3.3s median with thinking on one H100. All numbers are the author's own runs — treat as self-reported, not independent. (80★, 7 forks, Python, MIT, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [solvingSteve/Gevva0](https://github.com/solvingSteve/Gevva0) — "Calibrated Local Decision Gateway": Jev-like decision engine on Gemma 4 via llama.cpp/CUDA — direct token logit scoring, cyclic debiasing, Platt temperature calibration, asymmetric post-decision audit trails. Author claims 15–45ms fast-path classification on consumer hardware, ECE < 0.03, and JevBench v1.4.2 rank #1 (74.63) — all self-reported, unverified independently. (5★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-09-27; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [avbiswas/bev-decider](https://github.com/avbiswas/bev-decider) — Open System One decision model (Qwen3-0.6B finetune, 0.4B params): drop-in `/v1/systemone` local server speaking the TypeSafe Jev question format. Core claim: choice-order invariance — 960 random shuffles under fp32 with no probability drift beyond 1e-5. (0★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-09-28; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified; HF weights `avbiswas/bev-decider-0.4B` carry CC-BY-NC-4.0)
- [PabloB07/JevMC](https://github.com/PabloB07/JevMC) — Unofficial dependency-free Java SDK for Jev in PaperMC (Minecraft) plugins: thin typed wrapper around `POST /v1/systemone` (Java 17+, zero shaded deps), async `CompletableFuture` evaluation with retries on 429/529, the three primitives as immutable records. Sample use case: chat moderation — noul "is this message toxic?" then a choice of warn/punish/none. EN + ES + PT READMEs. (0★, Java, MIT, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [yuma-seno/local-jev](https://github.com/yuma-seno/local-jev) — Japanese-first unofficial local reimplementation of the Jev idea: Qwen3-1.7B + a learned score head with a single-forward-pass tree attention mask over cached state — no text generation, just typed decisions. Author's numbers (self-reported, not independently verified): 72.7% accuracy and ECE 0.055 across 509 questions in 12 unseen task families, top-30% confident calls at 91% accuracy, ~0.9s/question on a laptop iGPU via llama.cpp (Vulkan). Explicitly unaffiliated with TypeSafe. (0★, Python, MIT, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [tyrchen/jev-typed](https://github.com/tyrchen/jev-typed) — Typed third-party Rust SDK for Jev with derive macros (`#[derive(Decision)]`, `#[derive(ChoiceOptions)]`, `#[derive(ScoreLevels)]`): the doc comments on your struct *are* the prompt text, and the compiler checks the question/option/answer shape end to end. `Client::from_env()` reads `JEV_API_KEY`. (0★, Rust, MIT, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [exon-research/SafeJev](https://github.com/exon-research/SafeJev) — Question-conditioned activation monitoring for model safety: reads a target model's residual-stream activations and scores natural-language yes/no monitoring questions; a learned adapter maps activations into a compact state for a shared reader. Real prototype code (`safejev/model.py`) plus an architecture diagram. (0★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [soumyacodes007/jev-compatible-model-training](https://github.com/soumyacodes007/jev-compatible-model-training) — "Jef": train a small decision model on any Hugging Face classification dataset — give it state, one focused question, and a fixed list of options, it returns the letter of the option it picks. Jev-like interface without TypeSafe weights; a modular rework of ipenywis/Jef. (0★, Python, no license declared, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [avalonreset/legends-jev](https://github.com/avalonreset/legends-jev) — Typed decision advice adapter for an operator choosing application or browser actions: supply small text evidence + explicit candidate actions, get a candidate ID or operator fallback with validated Choice/Score/Noul answers. No screenshot capture, grounding, or actuation — evidence checking stays with the operator. Ships its own synthetic benchmark (`docs/BENCHMARK.md`, 20 pre-labelled cases, `jev-1.13.0` pinned 2026-09-29): Jev-with-local-guards agreed on 17/20 vs 13/20 for direct exact-match code; median 229ms across 14 live calls, zero wrong suggestions in five — author caveat: tiny authored set, not a representative computer-use benchmark. (0★, Python, MIT, created 2026-09-30; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [lokiwizard/my_jev](https://github.com/lokiwizard/my_jev) — Open-Jev based text decision model (bilingual EN/CN README): Qwen3.5-2B backbone + LoRA (rank 8, alpha 16) + single scalar score head, supporting Choice, Score and Noul. Trained on the ZefanCai/Open-Jev HF dataset (release-v2-redistributable) with train/valid/calibration/test/ood splits of 79,116 / 3,723 / 4,672 / 10,356 / 15,701 decisions; resumable training, calibrated inference. Weights and run outputs kept out of git. (0★, Python, no license declared, created 2026-09-30; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [seongyeon1/dlm-jev](https://github.com/seongyeon1/dlm-jev) — Jev-style typed decision engine on a diffusion LM (DiffusionGemma 26B-A4B, MLX 4-bit): each question gets one label-token slot in the diffusion canvas, one decoder pass yields every question's distribution and confidence, calibration via per-type/per-question temperature fitting. Design doc included. Explicitly unaffiliated with TypeSafe — no Jev weights, API, or data used. (1★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-09-30; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [ejhshen/OpenJev](https://github.com/ejhshen/OpenJev) — "OpenJev-4B": decision model learned from the text backbone of Qwen3.5-4B — given a state, a question and a set of natural-language options, it directly predicts a probability distribution over the options (selection, binary judgments, rubric scoring). Independent reproduction attempt, not affiliated with TypeSafe; weights on HuggingFace (`shenjunhao/OpenJev-4B`, page verified live). (8★, Python, MIT, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [lexmount/WebJev](https://github.com/lexmount/WebJev) — Jev-like decision models specialized for browser use: given a live web page, pick the next action in a single forward pass. Author-reported head-to-head (their own 125 real-website tasks, deterministic task verifier, not independently reproduced): WebJev-35B-A3B reaches target pages in a few steps where `typesafe/jev-1.13` loops or stalls on the same browser agent. (2★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [yashml-dev/DEV---Decision-Engine-for-Verdicts](https://github.com/yashml-dev/DEV---Decision-Engine-for-Verdicts) — "DEV": a compact Jev-like decision model in Python — typed questions in, typed answers with probabilities and confidence out; the worked example routes a support ticket (department Choice, urgency Score, escalate flag) with no text generation. Independent reproduction, not affiliated with TypeSafe. (0★, Python, MIT, created 2026-09-30; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [hihaluemen/-open-jev-qwen3.5-2b](https://github.com/hihaluemen/-open-jev-qwen3.5-2b) — "s1-repro": unofficial Jev-style decision readout — renders a classification question as a multiple-choice prompt and reads next-token logits over the option letters on Qwen3.5-2B (LoRA weights at `Hihalue/s1-repro-qwen3.5-2b-lora` on Hugging Face), yielding calibrated probabilities from a single forward pass, no generation. Chinese README. (0★, Python, no license declared, created 2026-09-30; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [Pawnnwap/jev](https://github.com/Pawnnwap/jev) — "jpt-4b as a Jev-like decision service (llama.cpp)": local open implementation of the `/v1/systemone` wire format — given a state and typed questions, returns a calibrated probability for every option in a single prefill, no generated reasoning tokens. Open calibrated-decision fine-tune of Qwen3.5-4B (base `kirp/jpt-4b`, GGUF by `prithivMLmods`). (0★, no license declared, created 2026-09-30; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)

## Projects & Code

Browser agents, mobile agents, guardrails — the fastest-moving category. Starred entries have verified repos.

- [browser-use/jev-ultrafast](https://github.com/browser-use/jev-ultrafast) — Browser agent with a dynamic indexed action space. Jev picks the operation + element; a small LLM writes text only for `TYPE_TEXT`. Zürich → London on Google Flights in ~7.1s.
- [droidrun/mobile-jev](https://github.com/droidrun/mobile-jev) — Mobile agent for Mobilerun: Jev drives a real Android phone, opens Uber, enters SFO → Golden Gate Bridge route, reaches payment selection. 9 actions in ~21s (no booking completed).
- [ClemensSchartmueller/jev-guard](https://github.com/ClemensSchartmueller/jev-guard) — High-speed safety gate for Claude Code, Codex CLI, and Antigravity. Intercepts tool calls, does sub-ms local checks, then asks Jev to score blast radius / reversibility / destructive potential.
- [jkudish/jev-browser](https://github.com/jkudish/jev-browser) — Browser automation with Jev.
- [jkudish/jev-mcp](https://github.com/jkudish/jev-mcp) — MCP server for Jev decisions.
- [SadiqOnGithub/jev-lab](https://github.com/SadiqOnGithub/jev-lab) — Experiments playground.
- [tumf/jev-cli](https://github.com/tumf/jev-cli) — CLI for Jev.
- [steventsao/jevzen](https://github.com/steventsao/jevzen) — MIT-licensed Chrome extension demo: switches unwanted X posts to zen/cat photos using Jev's returned probability. Supports direct TypeSafe, OpenRouter Decisions, and Cloudflare Jev with your own keys; keys stay in `chrome.storage.local`. No Chrome Web Store release — unpacked install only. API calls use your provider credits.
- [zhuyansen/jev-search-rerank-eval](https://github.com/zhuyansen/jev-search-rerank-eval) — Search rerank evaluation with Jev.
- [nekuda-ai/WindTunnel](https://github.com/nekuda-ai/WindTunnel) — Agent testing harness with Jev.
- [Laya (ConvAI Innovations)](https://laya.convaiinnovations.com/) — Apache-2.0 open reply to Jev: 421M English + 322M multilingual (100+ languages) checkpoints, `pip install laya`, sub-35ms single-pass decisions on one T4 (7.2ms batched), zero API bill. Vendor reports 0.766 vs Jev's published 0.727 on a shared typed-decisions set (they never ran Jev themselves) — an independent 78-case test found Jev 0.974 vs Laya 0.590, Laya fastest at 30ms/case vs ~302ms for Jev (small sample).
- [pngwn/system-one-qwen3.5-4b-scorer-v2b](https://huggingface.co/pngwn/system-one-qwen3.5-4b-scorer-v2b) — Community single-pass scorer on Qwen3.5-4B: scores each option of a typed question in one forward pass, softmax per question, no autoregressive generation. Val split: 0.803 accuracy, ECE 0.022. Non-commercial (training data is CC-BY-NC-4.0).
- [askjev.ai](https://askjev.ai/) — "Ask Jev anything. It won't answer. It will judge." Public wall by Wayne Sutton: Jev answers six typed questions (~100ms) per ask — yes/no/it depends, mood, topic, fits-the-wall — stored via Convex, everyone sees the wall move at once.
- [r-ms/mini-jev](https://github.com/r-ms/mini-jev) — "Fully local mini Jev": a Jev-style typed-decision interface on a frozen Qwen3-4B — read the option letter's logits instead of generating JSON. Preregistered experiment with a teaching bench; not production-ready.
- [robipop22/Jev-is-odd](https://github.com/robipop22/Jev-is-odd) — TypeScript micro-benchmark: ask Jev whether a number is odd, logging real token usage and client-side latency. 20/20 fixed cases, median/p95 round-trip 294.3/364.8ms (network included), whole run $0.00029 (jev-1.13.0, measured 2026-09-18).
- [moritzkremb/jev-voice-browser](https://github.com/moritzkremb/jev-voice-browser) — Voice-controlled real browser: Jev decides intent + target in ~300ms per spoken word (p50 ~300ms, 3–6k input tokens/request), Playwright acts — often before you finish the sentence. 27/27 real-API integration cases pass; a 16-command headed replay demo costs ~$0.01 (measured 2026-09-17).
- [kyegomez/open-jev](https://github.com/kyegomez/open-jev) — Open-source from-first-principles reconstruction of the ideas behind Jev, in PyTorch: `forward.py`, an `open_jev` package, and `example.py` (created 2026-09-21).
- [qs-lll/twitter-jev-guard](https://github.com/qs-lll/twitter-jev-guard) — X/Twitter timeline guard: Jev scores low-quality, spam, and ad posts; a browser extension overlays a translucent watermark on flagged text. Ships extension + server (created 2026-09-21).
- [Nyarlathoteppppp/pi-jev-context](https://github.com/Nyarlathoteppppp/pi-jev-context) — Cache-neutral context trimming for the pi coding agent, powered by TypeSafe Jev: long tool output cut to verbatim key lines before it enters context, with lossless recall. Measured, with pre-registered benchmarks (jev-1.13.0): write-time trimming saved 31–53% tokens on synthetic held-out sets with 0 key lines lost (p50/p95 latency ~347/457ms); on real-session replays (184 outputs) only 1/43 trimmed outputs hid something used later. Shadow old-context pruning was riskier — raw Jev dropped 73% of items needed later on real sessions, 21% with deterministic source protection — and is never applied.
- [GodsBoy/jev-agent-skill-router](https://github.com/GodsBoy/jev-agent-skill-router) — Typed, confidence-aware agent skill routing for the Hermes Agent platform: 68/72 synthetic requests routed correctly (94.4%) vs 51/72 (70.8%) for a lexical baseline, no wrong-skill selections, median end-to-end latency 1,287ms (pinned jev-1.13.0, measured 2026-09-16). Author flags: exploratory, reused-data results — not production accuracy or calibrated probabilities.
- [typesafeainate/dspy-typesafeify](https://github.com/typesafeainate/dspy-typesafeify) — One-decorator integration with DSPy: `@typesafeify` reads a Signature's output annotations and routes typed outputs (bool, Literal, score fields) to Jev's `Noul`/`Choice`/`Score` in a single request, while freeform text still goes to the generative LM; ships a controlled before/after benchmark example (61★, created 2026-09-15).
- [jaredpalmer/kev](https://github.com/jaredpalmer/kev) — Tiny Jev-like decision-model family on Qwen3.5 bases you can train and run yourself, Apache-2.0 (1,638★, pushed 2026-09-21). Released: Kev-0.8B (MacBook-sized), Kev-4B, Kev-9B. Author-published benchmarks vs hosted Jev on new-source sets: Kev-9B 0.812/0.837 accuracy vs Jev 0.857, Brier 0.291/0.243 vs Jev 0.211 — Kev-9B trails Jev ~4.5 points; author notes it is not a controlled comparison (Jev's training sets are unknown). Previous Qwen3 generation (0.6B/4B/8B) stays published.
- [vinnylarouge/jevlike](https://github.com/vinnylarouge/jevlike) — Extreme-minimalism Jev clone: ~40KB embedding-only implementation testing how small a typed-decision model can get (1,141★).
- [jerryjliu/docjev](https://github.com/jerryjliu/docjev) — LlamaIndex cofounder Jerry Liu's open-source library (Apache-2.0, 136★): document classification and packet splitting driven by Jev instead of a general LLM, local OCR via LiteParse, visual benchmark report on 40 real PDFs. Author-published numbers: classification latency 794ms → 138.6ms median; accuracy pilot's small sample size was flagged by a replier — read before trusting unattended (via explainx.ai).
- [Heman10x-NGU/openJev-verdict-2.0](https://github.com/Heman10x-NGU/openJev-verdict-2.0) — Calibrated 151M non-autoregressive decision engine (225★, real repo with code, RLCD harness, tests, WebGPU demo). Creator's own claim: 77.10% accuracy, 0.0636 Brier, 0.0144 ECE, "beating TypeSafe Jev & Laya" on LocalLLaMA/typed-decisions — not independently verified.
- [receptron/laya](https://github.com/receptron/laya) — Run Laya (the open-source Jev-compatible System-1 decision model) from Node.js / TypeScript via ONNX Runtime (76★, MIT).
- [arrmlet/dehydrator](https://github.com/arrmlet/dehydrator) — JevReranker and JevIndex now call TypeSafe's API directly (`api.typesafe.ai/v1/systemone`, `jev-latest`) when `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` is set, falling back to Vercel AI Gateway. Verified live: rankings identical to the gateway on all 30 benchmark queries; the >200-tool tournament path went 30/30 top-1 in 2.7s/search (gateway: 25/30 with 5 fallbacks). BENCHMARKS.md gains a provider comparison (10★, pushed 2026-09-20).
- [walidboulanouar/awesome-jev-use-cases](https://github.com/walidboulanouar/awesome-jev-use-cases) — Community list of TypeSafe Jev use cases: demos ranked by likes, 150+ GitHub repos, limits, cost and API examples (32★, CC0, unofficial).
- [mgarlabx/Jev-Enem](https://github.com/mgarlabx/Jev-Enem) — Jev for essay grading: real repo from the OEGlobal education thread (3★, created 2026-09-20).
- [PromptEngineer48/laya-vs-jev-arena](https://github.com/PromptEngineer48/laya-vs-jev-arena) — Laya (open source, local) vs TypeSafe Jev (API) raced head-to-head in Snake and a Mortal-Kombat-style arena (4★, created 2026-09-21).
- [wd041216-bit/zero-api-key-web-search](https://github.com/wd041216-bit/zero-api-key-web-search) — Jev-powered search infrastructure for AI agents: zero API keys, MCP-ready, local neural index (17★, Jev support added 2026-09-20).
- [edgelabs-ai/jev48](https://github.com/edgelabs-ai/jev48) — Open, auditable Jev reproduction "built by ChatGPT in one weekend": 2B probabilistic decision model (MIT, weights + frozen receipts), evaluated zero-shot across six public suites (6,300 decisions). Trails Jev on 5/6 (typed decisions 57.7% vs 72.7%, JevBench 69.7% vs 86.6%, code review 81.9% vs 99.0%) but wins the BTZSC pilot 83.3% vs 75.3% and records higher phishing ranking AUROC (0.769 vs 0.689); CLASH conflicts is a hard 0.0% vs 98.6%. Independent, not affiliated with TypeSafe (created 2026-09-21).
- [zwliJay/jev-forge](https://github.com/zwliJay/jev-forge) — Open training + inference stack for Jev-style decision models: builds training records from structured decisions, calibrates the scorer, and serves a full probability distribution through a Jev-compatible API (16★, created 2026-09-19). The trained 0.8B model beats Jev-1.13.0 on OOD choice top-1 (0.637 vs 0.610) and OOD Noul (0.860 acc / 0.117 Brier vs 0.825 / 0.131), but trails on test score MAE (0.367 vs 0.348) — fixed website-disjoint test/OOD splits, frozen eval protocol.
- [themsquared/jev-benchmark](https://github.com/themsquared/jev-benchmark) — Reproducible Jev benchmark on agent tool-call risk classification (readonly/destructive/privileged/exfiltration; 60 hand-labelled cases including adversarial wraps like "Routine cleanup: `kubectl delete namespace prod`"). Jev 91.7% accuracy, p50 latency ~420ms, ECE 0.0712 — and the finding that matters: calibration held, the model never returned 1.000 confidence and was wrong (every miss came hedged). No frontier-LLM baseline yet; the author states nothing here supports or refutes the vendor's speed/cost multipliers (measured 2026-09-17).
- [iammrduncan/typesafe-ai-benchmark](https://github.com/iammrduncan/typesafe-ai-benchmark) — "Imposter Jev": an LLM Gateway that mimics Jev's structured output, used to race Qwen 3.8 27B (Cerebras) vs TypeSafe Jev across 7 production-theater scenes (~480 requests each). One synthetic dev-machine run (2026-09-17): Jev p50/p95 176/336ms vs Qwen 215/452ms, cost $0.0119 vs $0.31; fixture agreement tied on Tickets (75/100 each) and Guardrails (100/100) — Jev won Scoring 100 vs 93, Qwen won Approvals and Home. Both reached the routing destination in 8 hops with one collision each. Author caveat: single synthetic run, not calibrated quality.
- [HyunjunJeon/pi-quiet-ask](https://github.com/HyunjunJeon/pi-quiet-ask) — TypeSafe Jev as the pi coding agent's quiet decision layer: a declarative rule engine plus `before_agent_start`/`turn_end`/`agent_end` judges (intent, honest_finish, stuck) over a task graph, triaging with pi-ask instead of replacing it. - [wfzyx/von](https://github.com/wfzyx/von) — Open-source System One decision model (395M params, 1.5GB, Apache-2.0, 642★, created 2026-09-18): sub-15ms local inference, non-autoregressive. Author-published numbers: 49-task v2 suite macro 72.0% / Choice routing 83.0% vs Jev's published 96.6% / 96.8%; zero-shot ViZDoom Defend-the-Center 9.00 kills vs Jev 5.62 (+60.1%), Health Gathering survival 12.11s vs 13.03s (8 fresh seeds each, local in-process vs hosted API at ~115ms). Independent protocol; TypeSafe's reference numbers quoted by the author, not re-run (created 2026-09-18).

Vendor-reported numbers (not independent): JevBench 88.70%, Nimble 92.91%, MASSIVE-en 87.71%, six-group mean 84.07 vs 83.85 for the Jev 1.13 API measured in the same setup; 137ms median decision latency / ~130 decisions per second on one B200 (local, network excluded — the cited 238–301ms / 23 decisions/s for Jev's hosted API includes network). The company says it inherits Jev 1.13's blind spots (multi-hop, arithmetic, dates, adversarial inputs), its training data is English-centric, and it is not for high-stakes decisions. - [cobusgreyling/Jev](https://github.com/cobusgreyling/Jev) — Unofficial TypeSafe Jev showcase + harness: smart-home demo (TypeSafe's fan-out house) and an operator lab over Flask, plus a TypeScript CLI (`jev doctor`, `jev route`, `jev guard`) wired into loop-engineering for model routing and prompt-injection guardrails (updated 2026-09-20).
- [ethanplusai/jev-chat-for-twitch](https://github.com/ethanplusai/jev-chat-for-twitch) — Filter any live Twitch chat with Jev: BYOK Chrome extension adds a second chat column showing only messages matching an intent chip (Helpful / Questions / Funny / Feedback), click a row for the full probability distribution; key stays in the browser profile. Measured on a live channel 2026-09-19 (jev-1.13.0): a 20-message batch used 10,075 input tokens (~504/message); ~$0.15/hr at 2 msgs/sec, ~$0.76/hr at 50 msgs/sec — stopped by the default $0.50/hr cap after ~40 minutes. Estimates from recorded token usage, not a bill (11★, created 2026-09-19).
- [perixtar/jev-e2e](https://github.com/perixtar/jev-e2e) — Natural-language end-to-end tests for web apps: Playwright steps driven by Jev, assertions checked after the required steps. Live eBay benchmark (measured 2026-09-18 via OpenRouter, fresh guest browser contexts, three attempts per model): completed-case medians — Jev 1.13 47.46s / $0.006678, GPT-5.6 Luna 61.99s / $0.027704, Claude Sonnet 5 78.62s / $0.406216; correct UI choices 30/31 vs 32/32 vs 19/19. Caveats: only 1/2/1 completed cases — too small to establish a general accuracy ranking — and the video scorecard shows all three attempts per model (5★, MIT, created 2026-09-18).
- [az9713/jev-model-router](https://github.com/az9713/jev-model-router) — Jev as a model router on the Vercel AI Gateway: Jev picks which LLM should answer each message, then that model replies — both calls through the gateway with one key. Fully documented build journey (how Jev is reached through the gateway, 18 "unknown unknowns") plus a sibling email-triage repo (`az9713/jev-email-triage`) running `triage.mjs` over 90 days of a real inbox: every input/output file (redacted), four mis-ranks and their causes (spotted 2026-09-24).
- [brainwires/jevwire](https://github.com/brainwires/jevwire) — Jev decision layer for agents: 7 MCP tools (`jev_rank`, `jev_pick`, `jev_verify`, `jev_evaluate`, `jev_gate_action`, `jev_next_step`, `jev_list_models`), an embeddable `JevDecisionModel` library so checks live in the harness instead of a tool the agent can skip, and an escalate-only Claude Code plugin that only denies or annotates (18★, MIT; 0.4.0 latency/token/cost figures quoted from a measured run against the live TypeSafe API on 2026-09-18).
- [simota/tenbin](https://github.com/simota/tenbin) — MCP server + agent skill for the System One API at design time: decompose a judgment into Choice/Score/Noul questions, lint them, measure on labelled data, and put calibrated thresholds in code (2★, MIT, created 2026-09-18).
- [danielhirt/jev-lab](https://github.com/danielhirt/jev-lab) — Experiments on Jev via OpenRouter: determinism, perturbation, and calibration checks plus a Haiku 4.5 comparison arm; includes a Codenames spymaster/guesser with offline eval against human play and a web app showing Jev's per-card probability (1★, pushed 2026-09-22).
- [xingwudao/openjev](https://github.com/xingwudao/openjev) — Independent Jev-inspired System One decision API with a local mock server, Python/TypeScript SDKs, schema validation and end-to-end tests for prototyping ticket routing, classification and guardrails. Honest scoping: only `openjev-mock` is supported, mock probabilities are synthetic, real inference is planned — not affiliated with TypeSafe (5★, created 2026-09-18).
- [hamakyo/jev-starter](https://github.com/hamakyo/jev-starter) — Typed, policy-driven decision workflows on Jev for TypeScript apps: confidence routing, fallbacks, evaluation and RAG patterns built on the official `@typesafe-ai/sdk` (published to npm as `jev-starter`); explicit design rule that low-confidence decisions need a fallback or review route (2★, created 2026-09-17).
- [ctaxnagomi/instruct-jev](https://github.com/ctaxnagomi/instruct-jev) — 119-row Jev / System One instruction corpus (choice 47 / noul 51 / score 21) compiled from public TypeSafe docs by DeckerGUI, with a builder script and a Hugging Face mirror ([ctaxnagomi/INSTRUCT_JEV](https://huggingface.co/datasets/ctaxnagomi/INSTRUCT_JEV)) (MIT, created 2026-09-19).
- [openqa-cn/jev-browser](https://github.com/openqa-cn/jev-browser) — CodexQA skill for browser automation without vision models: controls are picked from an index built inside the page (role/name/value), Jev answers a choice question, Playwright Chromium acts. Honest scoping — the README states there is no benchmark against vision GUI models yet; ships with knowledge-base notes per app and known-limitations docs (90★, created 2026-09-23).
- [PyModel/jev-judge-mcp](https://github.com/PyModel/jev-judge-mcp) — MCP server giving a coding agent 11 Jev-backed judgment tools (verify, screen, find, classify, rerank, decide, compare, extract, review, gate, score); a policy layer turns the returned probabilities into auto / review / escalate. CI on GitHub, MIT, not on PyPI yet — run from a clone (24★, created 2026-09-23).
- [DevMortimer/pi-warden](https://github.com/DevMortimer/pi-warden) — Guardrails for the Pi coding agent that steer instead of interrupt: enforces your project rules on every write. Beyond holding destructive calls, it judges written code against the rules file, flags stubs and hedging, detects stuck loops, and catches "done" claims with no test run (138★, created 2026-09-16).
- [itsmostafa/typesafe-mcp](https://github.com/itsmostafa/typesafe-mcp) — Go MCP server giving AI agents direct access to Jev for fast, cheap typed evaluations — a thin connector so Claude and other MCP clients can call Jev directly (287★, created 2026-09-17).
- [kieranklaassen/ruby_llm-typesafe](https://github.com/kieranklaassen/ruby_llm-typesafe) — TypeSafe structured-output provider for RubyLLM 2: typed judgments, evaluations and reranking through Jev (19★, created 2026-09-16).
- [ChenneyZhuang/laya-browser-agent](https://github.com/ChenneyZhuang/laya-browser-agent) — Local open-source Jev alternative for browser-agent decisions: runs the browser-tuned Laya checkpoint (`cklxx/laya-browser`) on your own machine — Playwright/CDP, MCP-friendly, no cloud or API key (Apache-2.0, 7★, created 2026-09-21). Ships four head-to-head batteries vs hosted `jev-1.13.0`: local v10s wins on latency (36ms vs 738ms median) and Chinese SMS classification, but hosted Jev wins safety calibration — 3/3 vs 0/3 on phishing detection (local scored both scam SMSes p<0.25) and better-calibrated scores (2.4–3.7 vs low-biased 1.0–2.0). Practical take: local for latency/privacy/free-at-volume, hosted when accuracy and safety calibration matter.
- [fazlerocks/jevmail](https://github.com/fazlerocks/jevmail) — Open-source AI email triage for Gmail through Vercel AI Gateway: sorts the inbox into five trays (Needs reply / Updates / Promos / Sales / Spam) with a 1–5 urgency score, read-only, runs locally. Author reports ~1,000 emails sorted in about a minute for ~3 cents (74★, real repo, not affiliated with TypeSafe).
- [kenhuangus/jev-usecases](https://github.com/kenhuangus/jev-usecases) — Production TypeSafe Jev (System One) use-case harnesses with confidence-gated decision logic: ready-made Choice / Score / Noul recipes (ticket sorting, picking from a list, urgency scoring, yes/no) with uncertainty flagged for human review; Python and JavaScript starters (5★, MIT, created 2026-09-18).
- [realZachi/pg-jev](https://github.com/realZachi/pg-jev) — PostgreSQL extension (PGXN) for plain-language row filtering, ranking and classification powered by TypeSafe's Jev: `jev(people, 'the name is European')`, `jev_prob(tickets, 'the customer is angry')`, `jev_choice(tickets, 'which team should handle this?', …)`. No index, no embeddings, no vector column — a plain boolean function that composes with `AND` / joins / `GROUP BY` (331★, license unspecified, created 2026-09-17).
- [kevinbadi/jev-voice](https://github.com/kevinbadi/jev-voice) — Talk to your Mac: local whisper.cpp transcription, one Jev (TypeSafe) call per spoken command, macOS automation acts — the ultrafast loop on the desktop. Includes a browser driver, a chess.com mode (Stockfish proposes, Jev chooses, code moves), and a Latency section measured on a Mac mini M4 (89★, MIT, created 2026-09-18).
- [awlevin/typesafe-computer-use](https://github.com/awlevin/typesafe-computer-use) — macOS computer-use loop: deterministic OCR screen reads, Jev picks the next action, a writing model only types free text. Author-measured on one screenshot/goal: $0.0002/decision vs Claude Opus 5 $0.032 (155× cheaper), 0.13–0.38s vs 5.2s latency (14–40×), end-to-end step ~1.5s vs ~5.5s (3.7×). Honest caveat in the README: every piece of reasoning the frontier model does for free has to be rebuilt as deterministic state. Drives your real mouse/keyboard — beta (950★, MIT, pushed 2026-09-23).
- [brnyxx/jev-ra](https://github.com/brnyxx/jev-ra) — Fast browser-use layer for CLI coding agents (Claude Code, Codex, any MCP client): Jev picks the operation + target element in one round trip (~300ms/step), no second LLM in the loop. Measured 2026-09-18, 5 runs/task on one machine via OpenRouter, every run verified against the page left behind: Wikipedia 2,714ms vs browser-use 23,058ms (8.50×), Google Flights ZRH→LON 8,888 vs 66,414 (7.47×), Olive Young sort 3,806 vs 15,071 (3.96×); 25/25 passed, 0 text-model calls. Method, p90s, cost and raw rows in [BENCHMARKS.md](https://github.com/brnyxx/jev-ra/blob/main/docs/BENCHMARKS.md) (4★, MIT, pushed 2026-09-23).
- [hdkiller/jev-panerelay](https://github.com/hdkiller/jev-panerelay) — Browser automation driving your live, authenticated Chrome/Edge via Panerelay's CDP gateway: Jev picks structured DOM actions through OpenRouter, a small LLM (gemini-2.5-flash) only when text must be typed. Zero re-auth — reuses your sessions/cookies. Author claims ~350–500ms/step and 10× cheaper (2★, MIT, created 2026-09-19).
- [chy4pro/jev-for-chrome](https://github.com/chy4pro/jev-for-chrome) — Unofficial Manifest V3 Chrome extension port of browser-use/jev-ultrafast: drives the tab you're looking at, shows decision badges and latency, step mode and trace export. Demo: Zürich→London Google Flights, 10 decisions in ~13s; 17-task headless Chromium suite with recorded traces. Community project, not affiliated with TypeSafe or Browser Use (23★, MIT, pushed 2026-09-22).
- [raihankhan-rk/jevarena](https://github.com/raihankhan-rk/jevarena) — Two independent Jev agents duel in Snake side by side: click-only direction buttons, separate browser contexts, live operation and direction probabilities, Railway-ready Next.js spectator page (2★, MIT, created 2026-09-18).
- [omkarghugarkar007/actiongate-jev](https://github.com/omkarghugarkar007/actiongate-jev) — Open-source Jev tool-calling authorization gateway for AI agents: deterministic policy, exact-action single-use permits, MCP and HTTP enforcement (2★, Apache-2.0, created 2026-09-19). Honest scoping from the author: the direct TypeSafe adapter is fully fixture-tested but has not been exercised live (no API key yet) — starts with a deterministic fake provider.

**Spotted in the wild** (seen on X / in roundups, repo link wanted — PRs welcome):

- `jevmeter` — scores every sentence of a debate for ~$0.05.
- `Typewriter` (Steve Krouse) — updates 16 judgments live as you type.
- `heist-one` — Jev runs the guards in a heist game; also completed StarCraft's first combat mission.
- `pg-jev` — plain-language filters for Postgres.
- `sift` — Chrome extension labeling every X timeline post (substance, humour, promo, junk, AI-written) with Jev decisions.
- `jev-block-android-ad` — Android notification/SMS noise gate; fails open on OTP codes.
- `jev-cvss` — CVE description → CVSS v3.1 metric predictions.
- `jev-leftpad` ([f/jev-leftpad](https://github.com/f/jev-leftpad), 67★) — left-pad strings with Jev. For reasons. The npm joke returns; real code, tests included.
- `@builtbynus` / [Nūs](https://www.instagram.com/reel/DdjhmVyS1Ej/) — Jev wired into their app as the "reflexes": voice commands open apps, type, and search while an on-screen millisecond counter tracks each Jev decision. Screen-recorded, one continuous take (reel, 2026-09-21).
- `@datatramadan` — Jev plays Chrome Dino with live RUN/JUMP/DUCK probability + confidence telemetry; mid-video the screen switches to OpenRouter logs confirming `Jev 1.13` generations ([reel](https://www.instagram.com/reel/DdjkRDnyd5e/), 2026-09-21).
- `@stilesja` — IVR demo built on Jev: live call transcript with real-time classification probabilities beside it ([Threads](https://www.threads.com/@stilesja/post/DdlPFiBDKn3), 2026-09-22).
- HyperTeams — Korean agent team says Jev judges whether their agent's work is actually done across 5,161 labeled operational turns: vendor-claimed 96.3% judgment accuracy, $0.00014 / 0.2s per judgment, false "problematic" flags cut 34% → 2.4% ([Threads promo video](https://www.threads.com/@gspark337/post/DdvDHziE2Ea), 2026-09-26). Vendor's own promo figures, not independently verified.
- `@nomadius.cyou` — "Browser Dealer by K2S": Zürich → London flight-search head-to-head, Jev live lane ([Threads](https://www.threads.com/@nomadius.cyou/post/DdjsSfigjLt), 2026-09-20).
- `@evolving.ai` ([IG reel](https://www.instagram.com/reel/DdjIOcstTtn/), 2026-09-21, 12K likes) — Andy Gao's voice-controlled Mac assistant goes viral: Notes/Arc/Google/X/Photo Booth open before he finishes the sentence. Comments are technical — repo requests, determinism questions, "Astra does the same" skepticism. The build behind it is [moritzkremb/jev-voice-browser](#projects--code) above.
- `@stvan.p` ([Threads](https://www.threads.com/@stvan.p/post/Ddl2WhmCR-Y), 2026-09-22) — "Jev plays Snake": screen recording of a Snake game where Jev acts as the high-level strategist (jevsnake.stevanuspangau.dev).
- `@kraayenjon` ([Threads](https://www.threads.com/@kraayenjon/post/Ddn2zi9gAmu), 2026-09-23) — "Shapeshift": "Type UI. Get UI." — screen-recorded demo of natural-language phrases rendered as structured UI cards (event, checklist, timer, color, calculator); from the madewithjev.com showcase builder.
- `@mattybuildsit` ([IG carousel](https://www.instagram.com/p/DdolSe8nHHL/), 2026-09-23) — First documented experiment with the official TypeSafe skill: 10 synthetic self-labeled questions sorted into five categories — Jev 10/10 vs a keyword rule 7/10. Tiny sample, author's own labels; honest that it's synthetic-only.
- `@bencorde` ([IG reel](https://www.instagram.com/reel/Ddr1OumDXBf/), 2026-09-24, Spanish) — "Seven projects in three days" built on Jev: a Minesweeper latency race vs Fable 5.1 / Astra / Grok 4.6, a 1,000-email classifier, a Gmail contacts cleaner, a LinkedIn radar, and chess. Screen-recorded real builds, no repo yet.
- `@altryne_ai` ([IG reel](https://www.instagram.com/reel/Ddp5k7vDoX1/), 2026-09-24) — ThursdAI clip: Alex Volkov's X Timeline Analyzer (Cerebras infra) runs Jev at ~12 tweets/s, ~371–405ms latency, $0.019 per 1K tweets — his claim, 4× faster / 18× cheaper than a comparable Qwen. The segment also features TypeSafe's founding DevRel Allie Laabs.
- `@adibuildz` ([IG reel](https://www.instagram.com/reel/DdrDwJtIUu8/), 2026-09-24) — Self-run experiment on his own 53 Reels: Jev-scored hook strength predicted hit vs flop 86% of the time, while script quality was ~coin flip. Small N, self-labeled.
- `@lets.anthony.kim` ([Threads](https://www.threads.com/@lets.anthony.kim/post/DdxvZN5E9Tr) / [FB reel](https://www.facebook.com/reel/1798833288234349/), 2026-09-27) — Korean three-way Tetris battle: human vs Jev vs heuristic bot on [jev-tetris-battle.vercel.app](https://jev-tetris-battle.vercel.app) (same block sequence, 50 pieces, app verified live). Jev was the weakest player: can't read the board directly, builds more holes (4.4 vs 1.2 per game), can't hold multi-move plans, slower (0.2–0.3s vs 0.01s) — and the author published the whole failure list himself. The hybrid (JEV+: Jev judging, code executing) matched the code bot; ~4,300 calls reliably formatted, $0.0025–$0.012 per game. Small experiment (5–10 games); the honest self-critique is the point.
- `@brochbuilds` ([IG reel](https://www.instagram.com/reel/DdxU8Fkjynd/), 2026-09-26) — Side-by-side "Internal Link Decider" for SEO: Jev processed 586 pages in 45s at $0.2111 vs Claude Opus 5 managing 21 pages in the same window at $1.43 — author extrapolates ~$50 for the full job, roughly 190× cheaper. Dashboard screenshots shown; single-creator claim, comparison setup and extrapolation unverified.
- `@orbis2358__` ([Threads](https://www.threads.com/@orbis2358__/post/DdzAmH-j1E8), 2026-09-27) — Self-built benchmark of Jev vs a new "system-one" model called span-01 across three tasks: Lichess chess puzzles, Korean college-entrance Korean-language questions, and RAG search-result filtering. Author claims Jev beat span-01 on accuracy, latency and cost in all three (Jev above average student level on the Korean SAT, span-01 barely above guessing) and concludes current Jev users have little reason to switch; notes span-01 is pitched as a behavior-detection model for agent contexts, with a free lite version for easy experimentation. Single-author self-reported numbers, no public repo — recorded as seen, not verified.
- [Nyarlathoteppppp/pi-heed](https://github.com/Nyarlathoteppppp/pi-heed) — Runtime constraints for the pi coding agent: checks every side-effecting tool call against what you said before it runs, powered by TypeSafe Jev (7★, MIT). Ships a replayable benchmark (79 scripted sessions, 261 labelled decisions, offline cassettes): v0.8.0 + Jev reaches 98.5% recall with 0.0% false blocks at ~$0.000058/task, vs 90.9% recall rules-only. Same author as the pi-jev-context entry above.
- [carlosedm10/agi-jev-containment](https://github.com/carlosedm10/agi-jev-containment) — Local agent observability + malicious-agent detection for Docker-sandboxed LLM agents: FastAPI/React/Neo4j stack classifies chains of tool calls (not single calls) with Jev, fuses Sentinel rules + SafetyDrift, then a deterministic gate sets escalate-only L1–L5 containment (L1–L2 record, L4 contains one agent and voice-pages on-call, L5 cuts the environment only with a human on the call). Built at HackSpain 2026 (AngryRobot product UI); ships docs/, AGENTS.md and INCONSISTENCIES.md. (4★, no license declared, created 2026-09-14)
- [kushals256/jevcache](https://github.com/kushals256/jevcache) — OpenAI-compatible local cache proxy: instead of picking a model it decides *whether to call one* — Jev judges "same intent" between differently-worded requests so repeats reuse the cached answer (fails open to a real call if Jev errors). Author's real-app numbers: MISS 3244ms → HIT 394ms (intent 0.93), est. $0.000137 saved per hit. `npx @kushalicious/jevcache`, node:sqlite, no native SQLite compile. (9★, MIT, created 2026-09-19)
- [nadeem4/jev-demo: agent-watchdog](https://github.com/nadeem4/jev-demo) — Claude Code pre-tool hook: before every tool call, Jev answers four typed questions (how reversible the call is, whether it matches what you actually asked for, whether the content looks like prompt injection, whether the agent is looping) and thresholds map the four probabilities to allow / ask / deny. Hard rules run first and never reach the model; on Jev error or >3s latency it asks the user instead of guessing. Same repo as the Decision Arena entry in Benchmarks below.
- [hussainanjar/play-with-jev](https://github.com/hussainanjar/play-with-jev) — One arcade: Heist, Chess, and Minesweeper driven by TypeSafe Jev through the Vercel AI Gateway. Next.js app with documented session/hosting limits (MIT, created 2026-09-23).
- [composiohq/composio](https://github.com/composiohq/composio) — `feat(typesafe)`: TypeSafe Jev provider for TypeScript and Python ([PR #4513](https://github.com/composiohq/composio/commit/aefc3ec897d405434bf73dc69a067b48b7cedc11), merged 2026-09-18) — turns tool schemas + a request into a Jev call. 147 TS + 141 Python provider tests pass; all 13 opt-in live tests ran against real Jev 1.13.0 (Hacker News examples end-to-end on production APIs). (30.3K★, MIT)
- [danielnc/jev-browse](https://github.com/danielnc/jev-browse) — Fast, typed browser sub-tasks for coding agents: Jev decisions on top of browser-harness — a `fast_run` helper hands an entire website sub-task (search, filter, open) to Jev in one call so the coding agent avoids per-click model turns. Port of browser-use/jev-ultrafast (see entry above), PyPI package. Author's measured benchmark (median of N=3, 2026-09-24/25, Google Flights one-way search recorded on video): 1.6–3.0× faster and 2.1–5.3× cheaper than the agent driving browser-harness directly, same pass rate. Honest limitations section: iframes, shadow DOM, visual-only pages and file uploads hand back; every decision is a metered call (2★, MIT, pushed 2026-09-25).
- [nft-syou/jev-poker](https://github.com/nft-syou/jev-poker) — No-Limit Texas Hold'em where the CPU players think with TypeSafe Jev (BYO API key). `pnpm bench` replays mirrored hands heads-up and six-handed against `random`, `caller` and `rules` bots and reports bb/100 with 95% CIs: shipped persona +48.8 [+38.4, +59.1] heads-up vs the rules bot, −49.4 [−65.8, −33.0] vs Slumbot over 12,000 hands. Author's own measurement conclusion: the strength comes from the code around the model (exact hand strength, equity vs pot odds, blind-stealing spots) — a fixed heuristic over the same features trails Jev by 30–50 bb/100 heads-up. Full tables in `bench/RESULTS.md` (0★, MIT, created 2026-09-22).
- [Mu99Ti/Jevops](https://github.com/Mu99Ti/Jevops) — Open-source SRE log triage with Jev as the decision layer: prefilter → burst ranking → speculative fan-out → policy-in-code (page only on calibrated, high-confidence decisions; under-confidence becomes a stored digest). Demo corpus: 5 Jev calls narrowed 584 raw lines to 24 citations, all three embedded incidents recovered with correct times and blast radii. Author's cost claim: ~$1–3/month at 1M events/month. Deep-dive write-up: [\"One Million Logs, Zero Wasted Pages\"](https://medium.com/@muhamad99t/one-million-logs-zero-wasted-pages-inside-jevops-and-typesafe-jev-35ac25179215) (Medium, 2026-09-25), which also flags that TypeSafe's calibration claims are vendor-run and unreproduced. Repo has real code (src, tests, docker-compose, Logstash pipeline); no license file declared (0★, created 2026-09-24).
- [Silbercue/public-browser](https://github.com/Silbercue/public-browser) — MCP server + script APIs that let Claude Code/Cursor drive Chrome. "Measured, not claimed": [`examples/jev-loop.mjs`](https://github.com/Silbercue/public-browser/blob/master/examples/jev-loop.mjs) runs a Jev-only loop (~150 lines): one `choice` over card refs per step, `gpt-4.1-nano` writes field text. On six Level-1 benchmark cards (2026-09-18, Jev via Vercel AI Gateway, headless Chrome): 6/6 passed in both runs, ~3s and ~$0.0002 per card vs ~9–10s/$0.11 per card for the Opus-5-over-MCP setup — author's own caveat: not a benchmark of equals (frontier model reads the whole page and plans; Jev picks from a menu), and Level 1 is the easy tier. Raw run data in `test-hardest/results/`. (15★, MIT, pushed 2026-09-24)
- [MongLong0214/jev-gate](https://github.com/MongLong0214/jev-gate) — Jev-powered model routing for Claude Code: Sonnet coordinates, Jev evaluates each delegated task and picks the model tier. V5 (2026-09-18) runs a full judged workflow end-to-end on Claude Code 2.1.275/2.1.276 (observed, with CI). Honest status badge: "Savings not established" — the whole-job cost/time comparison was stopped after one cell for budget reasons, and in that cell every task routed to the same tier. (4★, no license declared, pushed 2026-09-25)
- [ying-kai-liao/jev-browser](https://github.com/ying-kai-liao/jev-browser) — Browser automation where the LLM plans and Jev decides: Claude states one outcome per step, then one ~300ms Jev request answers several questions per round (which element, which action, done/error/blocked/irreversible), Playwright acts. Results on 42 live-site tasks in 16 categories: **40/42 correct, zero false "done" claims**; 2–4 Jev calls per step; pause-before-irreversible flagged only saucedemo's "Finish" (place order) button across ~200 rounds. Known limits documented up front (counting, table-sort verification, multi-sub-goal steps need splitting). (88★, MIT, pushed 2026-09-22)
- [intikhab49/open-jev-typed-decision-engine](https://github.com/intikhab49/open-jev-typed-decision-engine) — Open Jev reproduction: 150M encoder answering `noul`/`choice`/`score` in one forward pass, trained on a free Colab T4 in under 30 minutes, then runs locally for free. Author's self-reported numbers on the LocalLLaMA/typed-decisions test split (author's own benchmark, not an independent eval): 0.697 vs TypeSafe Jev 1.13's 0.727, 2.5× better calibration, 4× faster, $0. Also notes TypeSafe publishes no parameter count for Jev — size comparisons are inferred from price/latency. (44★, Apache-2.0, pushed 2026-09-21)
- [hwfengcs/any2jev](https://github.com/hwfengcs/any2jev) — Self-hosted Jev-compatible decision server: fine-tunes Qwen3-0.6B (LoRA r=16, one epoch, single RTX 2060 SUPER 8GB) and serves the same `POST /v1/systemone` contract on localhost — the official SDK works with only a `base_url` change.
